<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the value of $x$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {x} {24} &= \dfrac {2} {3} \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {2 \times 24} {3} \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 8} {3} \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {3} \times 8} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
x               &= 2 \times 8 \\\\
x               &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$

x               &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 8} {3} \\\\
x               &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {3} \times 8} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
x               &= 2 \times 8 \\\\
x               &= 16
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the value of $y$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {2} {3}    &= \dfrac {50} {y} \\\\
2y                &= 50 \times 3 \\\\
y                 &= \dfrac {50 \times 3} {2} \\\\
y                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 25 \times 3} {2} \\\\
y                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 25 \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y                 &= 25 \times 3 \\\\
y                 &= 75  
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If my answer is $41$, what number did I start with?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number I started with $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 6 -7                     &= 41 \\\\
a \times 6                        &= 41 + 7 \\\\
a \times 6                        &= 48 \\\\
a                                 &= \dfrac{48} {6} \\\\
a                                 &= \dfrac{6 \times 8} {6} \\\\
a                                 &= \dfrac{\cancel 6 \times 8} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                 &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

The number I started with $= 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>

$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}
a \div 8    &= 72 \\\\
a \times 8  &= 72 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {72} {8} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {8 \times 9} {8} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 9} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a           &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

She works out $c \div c$ as $144$. What is the value of $c$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
c \div c    &= 144 \\\\
c \times c  &= 144 \\\\
c ^ 2       &= 144 \\\\
c           &= \sqrt {144} \\\\
c           &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

If she works out $b \times 4 + 4$ she gets $4$.
What is the value of $b$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

Using BIDMAS

$
\begin{aligned}
b \times 4 + 4    &= 4 \\\\
b \div 4 - 4      &= 4 \\\\
b \div 4          &= 4 + 4 \\\\
b \div 4          &= 8 \\\\
b                 &= 8 \times 4 \\\\
b                 &= 32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$32$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The rule  $a * b$ means $a \times a + b \times b$

That is, you multiply the first number by itself, multiply the second number by itself and then add the answers together.

For example, 

$
\begin{aligned}
2 * 3 &= 2 \times 2 + 3 \times 3 \\\\
      &= 4          + 9          \\\\
      &= 13     
\end{aligned}
$

And

$
\begin{aligned}
1 * 2 &= 1 \times 1 + 2 \times 2 \\\\
      &= 1          + 4          \\\\
      &= 5     
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
a * 1                         &= 65 \\\\
a \times a + 1 \times 1       &= 65 \\\\
a ^ 2 + 1                     &= 65 \\\\
a ^ 2                         &= 65 - 1 \\\\
a ^ 2                         &= 64 \\\\
a                             &= \sqrt {64} \\\\
a                             &= 8
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='answer'>

$8$

a ^ 2                         &= 65 - 1 \\\\
a ^ 2                         &= 64 \\\\

a                             &= 8
\end {aligned}

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>solihull-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:3:4</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Kamu Rohith</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
<li>
tcoin
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

There is a number of coins on a table. One quarter of them are showing heads. If I turn over two more coins to show heads then one third of the coins now show heads. How many coins are on the table?  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of coins $= c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {c} {3}                                                              &= \dfrac {c} {4} + 2 \\\\ 
\dfrac {c} {3} - \dfrac {c} {4}                                             &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {c \times 3} {4 \times 3}         &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {4c} {12} - \dfrac {3c} {12}                                         &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {4c - 3c} {12}                                                       &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c} {12}                                                             &= 2 \\\\
c                                                                           &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
c                                                                           &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

Number of coins on the table $= 24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of coins $= c$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {c} {3}                                                              &= \dfrac {c} {4} + 2 \\\\ 
\dfrac {c} {3} - \dfrac {c} {4}                                             &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {c \times 3} {4 \times 3}         &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {4c} {12} - \dfrac {3c} {12}                                         &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {4c - 3c} {12}                                                       &= 2 \\\\
\dfrac {c} {12}                                                             &= 2 \\\\
c                                                                           &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
c                                                                           &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of coins on the table $= 24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
2x + 7            &= 4x - 15 \\\\
2x - 4x           &= -15 - 7 \\\\
-2x               &= -22 \\\\
\cancel {-} 2x    &= \cancel {-} 22 \\\\
2x                &= 22 \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {22} {2} \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 11} {2} \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 11} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
x                 &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

x                 &= \dfrac {22} {2} \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 11} {2} \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 11} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
x                 &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

Part d)

move the 2x to RHS and -15 to LHS and then solve accordingly so that there will be no need of canceling the minus sign.

22 = 2x

2x = 22

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What sum of money did they receive __altogether__ in April if Peter received $\pounds 60$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money Helen received}   &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {2 \times 30} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 30} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 30 \\\\
\text {Money John received}   &= \dfrac {30} {5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {5 \times 6} {5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 6} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 6 \\\\
\text{Total money}            &= 60 + 30 + 6 \\\\
                              &= \pounds 96
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 96$

</div>

                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 30} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 30 \\\\
\text {Money John received}   &= \dfrac {30} {5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {5 \times 6} {5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 6} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 6 \\\\
\text{Total money}            &= 60 + 30 + 6 \\\\
                              &= \pounds 96
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If they received $\pounds 160$ altogether in May, how much did __John__ receive that month?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the money John received (in $\pounds$) $= a$

Money Helen received $= 5a$

Money Peter received $= 10a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 5a + 10a      &= \pounds 160 \\\\
16a               &= 160 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {160} {16} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {10 \times 16} {16} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {16}} {\cancel {16}} \\\\
a                 &= \pounds 10
\end{aligned}
$

Money John received $= \pounds 10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 10$

</div>
</div>

a                 &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {16}} {\cancel {16}} \\\\
a                 &= \pounds 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Money John received $= \pounds 10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>EDB8D422-9368-4C8B-8C79-7E41BBCC1FC9</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>solihull-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2:1:17</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Kamu Rohith</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_kr_g0</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prpcr</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

One quarter of the people at a party are men, a half are girls, one sixth are women, and there are five boys. How many people were at the party? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of people in the party $= a$

Number of men in the party $= \dfrac {a} {4}$

Number of girls in the party $= \dfrac {a} {2}$

Number of women in the party $= \dfrac {a} {6}$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {4} + \dfrac {a} {2} + \dfrac {a} {6} + 5                                                                                            &= a \\\\
\dfrac {a} {4} + \dfrac {a} {2} + \dfrac {a} {6} - a                                                                                            &= - 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3} + \dfrac {a \times 6} {2 \times 6} + \dfrac {a \times 2} {6 \times 2} - \dfrac {a \times 12} {1 \times 12}     &= -5 \\\\
\dfrac {3a} {12} + \dfrac {6a} {12} + \dfrac {2a} {12} - \dfrac {12a} {12}                                                                      &= -5 \\\\
\dfrac {3a + 6a + 2a - 12a} {12}                                                                                                                &= -5 \\\\
\dfrac {11a - 12a} {12}                                                                                                                         &= -5 \\\\
{}- \dfrac {a} {12}                                                                                                                             &= -5 \\\\
\cancel {-} \dfrac {a} {12}                                                                                                                     &= \cancel {-} 5 \\\\
a                                                                                                                                               &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                                                                                               &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

Total number of people $= 60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of people in the party $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of men in the party $= \dfrac {a} {4}$
<emptyline>
Number of girls in the party $= \dfrac {a} {2}$
<emptyline>
Number of women in the party $= \dfrac {a} {6}$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {4} + \dfrac {a} {2} + \dfrac {a} {6} + 5                                                                                            &= a \\\\
\dfrac {a} {4} + \dfrac {a} {2} + \dfrac {a} {6} - a                                                                                            &= - 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3} + \dfrac {a \times 6} {2 \times 6} + \dfrac {a \times 2} {6 \times 2} - \dfrac {a \times 12} {1 \times 12}     &= -5 \\\\
\dfrac {3a} {12} + \dfrac {6a} {12} + \dfrac {2a} {12} - \dfrac {12a} {12}                                                                      &= -5 \\\\
\dfrac {3a + 6a + 2a - 12a} {12}                                                                                                                &= -5 \\\\
\dfrac {11a - 12a} {12}                                                                                                                         &= -5 \\\\
{}- \dfrac {a} {12}                                                                                                                             &= -5 \\\\
\cancel {-} \dfrac {a} {12}                                                                                                                     &= \cancel {-} 5 \\\\
a                                                                                                                                               &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                                                                                               &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total number of people $= 60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='review-comments'>

<h4>Review Comments</h4>




Switch the LHS and RHS so that there is no need to cancel the - operator.

i.e Start like below

a = a/4 + a/2 + a/6 + 5

Then solve it accordingly.

<div class='question question'>

The rule $a * b$ means $(a \times a) + (b \times b)$
 
That is, you multiply the first number by the first number, the second number by the second number and then you add your answers together.

For example, 

$
\begin{aligned}
6 * 4 &= (6 \times 6) + (4 \times 4) \\
      &= 36           + 16           \\
      &= 52     
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='workings'>

&= (3 \times 3) + (2 \times 2) + (1 \times 1) + (0 \times 0) \\\\
&= 9 + 4 + 1 + 0 \\\\
&= 14
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$a * 8 = 208$. Work out the value of $a$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
a * 8                         &= 208 \\\\
(a \times a) + (8 \times 8)   &= 208 \\\\
a^2 + 64                      &= 208 \\\\
a^2                           &= 208 - 64 \\\\
a^2                           &= 144 \\\\
a                             &= \sqrt {144} \\\\
a                             &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

a                             &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$b * 2 = 4b$. Work out the value of $b$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
b * 2                                     &= 4b \\\\
(b \times b) + (2 \times 2)               &= 4b \\\\
b^2 + 4                                   &= 4b \\\\
b^2 - 4b + 4                              &= 0 \\\\
b^2 - 2b - 2b + 4                         &= 0 \\\\
b \times (b - 2) - 2 \times (b - 2)       &= 0 \\\\
(b-2) \times (b -2)                       &= 0 \\\\
(b-2)^2                                   &= 0 \\\\
b - 2                                     &= 0 \\\\
b                                         &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

b^2 + 4                                   &= 4b \\\\
b^2 - 4b + 4                              &= 0 \\\\
b^2 - 2b - 2b + 4                         &= 0 \\\\
b \times (b - 2) - 2 \times (b - 2)       &= 0 \\\\
(b-2) \times (b -2)                       &= 0 \\\\
(b-2)^2                                   &= 0 \\\\
b - 2                                     &= 0 \\\\
b                                         &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$\dfrac {3} {8}$  of a number is $27$. What is $\dfrac {1} {2}$ of the number? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {8} \ \text {of} \ a     &= 27 \\\\
\dfrac {3} {8} \times a             &= 27 \\\\
a                                   &= 27 \times \dfrac {8} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {27 \times 8} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times 8} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 9 \times 8} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                                   &= 9 \times 8 \\\\
a                                   &= 72
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 72$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {of} \ a \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 72 \\\\
&= \dfrac {72} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 36} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 36} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
&= 36
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

a                                   &= \dfrac {27 \times 8} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times 8} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 9 \times 8} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                                   &= 9 \times 8 \\\\
a                                   &= 72
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 72$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {of} \ a \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 72 \\\\
&= \dfrac {72} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 36} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 36} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
&= 36
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Find two whole numbers which multiply together to give $18$ and a difference of $7$. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first whole number $= a$

Let the second whole number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a \times b  &= 18 \\\\
a - b       &= 7 \\\\
a           &= 7 + b
\end {aligned}
$

We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin {aligned}
a \times b              &= 18 \\\\
(7 + b) \times b        &= 18 \\\\
7b + b ^ 2              &= 18 \\\\
b ^ 2 + 7b - 18         &= 0 \\\\
b ^ 2 + 9b - 2b - 18    &= 0 \\\\
b (b + 9) -2 (b + 9)    &= 0 \\\\
(b - 2) (b + 9)         &= 0 \\\\
b -2                    &= \dfrac {0} {b - 9} \\\\
b - 2                   &= 0 \\\\
b                       &= 2
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {The second whole number}     &= 2 \\\\
\text {The first whole number}      &= 7 + b \\\\
                                    &= 7 + 2 \\\\
                                    &= 9
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first whole number $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the second whole number $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a \times b  &= 18 \\\\
a - b       &= 7 \\\\
a           &= 7 + b
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a \times b              &= 18 \\\\
(7 + b) \times b        &= 18 \\\\
7b + b ^ 2              &= 18 \\\\
b ^ 2 + 7b - 18         &= 0 \\\\
b ^ 2 + 9b - 2b - 18    &= 0 \\\\
b (b + 9) -2 (b + 9)    &= 0 \\\\
(b - 2) (b + 9)         &= 0 \\\\
b -2                    &= \dfrac {0} {b - 9} \\\\
b - 2                   &= 0 \\\\
b                       &= 2
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {The second whole number}     &= 2 \\\\
\text {The first whole number}      &= 7 + b \\\\
                                    &= 7 + 2 \\\\
                                    &= 9
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Jessica hires a boat from a Kiosk in the park. The owner of the boat charges $\pounds 2$ to take the boat and then $\pounds 3$ for each hour that it is used. If Jessica uses the boat for half an hour she only needs to pay half the hourly cost.

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total cost}      &= \pounds 2 + 2 \times \pounds 3 \\\\
                        &= 2 + 6 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 8
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If Jessica pays $\pounds 15.50$ for how long has she hired the boat?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the time she hired the boat $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\pounds 2 + a \times \pounds 3      &= \pounds 15.50 \\\\
3a                                  &= 15.50 - 2 \\\\
3a                                  &= 13.50 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {13.50} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= 4.50 \\\\
a                                   &= 4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {hours}
\end {aligned}
$

She hired the boat for $= 4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {hours}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {hours}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the time she hired the boat $= a$

a                                   &= \dfrac {13.50} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= 4.50 \\\\
a                                   &= 4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {hours}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
She hired the boat for $= 4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {hours}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {hours}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

In a particular kind of arithmetic  $a \circ b$ means multiply $a$ by itself and add the result to the answer you get if you multiply $b$ by itself.

So, for example

$
\begin{aligned} 
&= 3 \circ 5 \\\\
&= 3 \times 3 + 5 \times 5 \\\\
&= 9 + 25 \\\\
&= 34
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out $\dfrac {1} {2} \circ  \dfrac {1} {2}$         

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \circ \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {2} + \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1 \times 1} {2 \times 2} + \dfrac {1 \times 1} {2 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {4} + \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1 + 1} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2} {2 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{2}} {\cancel{2} \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \circ \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {2} + \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1 \times 1} {2 \times 2} + \dfrac {1 \times 1} {2 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {4} + \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1 + 1} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2} {4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2} {2 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{2}} {\cancel{2} \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find a positive value of $c$ that works in the following:

$c \circ c = 50$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned} 
c \circ c               &= 50 \\\\
c \times c + c \times c &= 50 \\\\
c^{2} + c^{2}           &= 50 \\\\
2c^{2}                  &= 50 \\\\
c^{2}                   &= \dfrac {50} {2} \\\\
c^{2}                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 25} {2} \\\\
c^{2}                   &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 25} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
c^{2}                   &= 25 \\\\
c                       &= \sqrt {25} \\\\
c                       &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned} 
c \circ c               &= 50 \\\\
c \times c + c \times c &= 50 \\\\
c^{2} + c^{2}           &= 50 \\\\
2c^{2}                  &= 50 \\\\
c^{2}                   &= \dfrac {50} {2} \\\\
c^{2}                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 25} {2} \\\\
c^{2}                   &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 25} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
c^{2}                   &= 25 \\\\
c                       &= \sqrt {25} \\\\
c                       &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

The temperature of water in a tank is decreasing at $3 \ \degree \ C$ per minute. If the bath starts with a temperature of $48 \ \degree \ C$ , after how long will the temperature reach $15 \ \degree \ C$? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the time taken (in $\text {minutes$) $= a$

$
\begin {aligned}
48 \degree \text {C}  - a \times 3 \degree \text {C}  &= 15 \degree \text {C} \\\\
48 - 3a                                               &= 15 \\\\
{}-3a                                                 &= 15 - 48 \\\\
{}-3a                                                 &= -33 \\\\
\cancel {-}  3a                                       &= \cancel {-} 33 \\\\
3a                                                    &= 33 \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac {33} {3} \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 11} {3} \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 11} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                                                     &= 11 \ \text {minutes}
\end {aligned}
$

Time taken to reach $15 \degree \text {C}$ $= 11 \ \text {minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11 \ \text {minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the time taken (in $\text {minutes$) $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
48 \degree \text {C}  - a \times 3 \degree \text {C}  &= 15 \degree \text {C} \\\\
48 - 3a                                               &= 15 \\\\
{}-3a                                                 &= 15 - 48 \\\\
{}-3a                                                 &= -33 \\\\
\cancel {-}  3a                                       &= \cancel {-} 33 \\\\
3a                                                    &= 33 \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac {33} {3} \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 11} {3} \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 11} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                                                     &= 11 \ \text {minutes}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Time taken to reach $15 \degree \text {C}$ $= 11 \ \text {minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11 \ \text {minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A theatre has seats arranged in rows of $22$. If an audience of $483$ fills the rows one by one, leaving no empty seats, how many rows will be full? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of rows fully occupied}     &= \dfrac {\text{Total audience}} {\text{Seats per row}} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {483} {22} \\\\
                                          &= 21 \ \text {remainder} \ 21 \\\\
                                          &\approx 21
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of rows fully occupied}     &= \dfrac {\text{Total audience}} {\text{Seats per row}} \\\\
                                          &= \dfrac {483} {22} \\\\
                                          &= 21 \ \text {remainder} \ 21 \\\\
                                          &\approx 21
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Sholto has $40$ helium balloons. Some of the balloons are red, some are blue and some are green. There are three times as many green balloons as there are red balloons and there are twice as many blue balloons as there are green balloons. 

How many balloons are blue? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of red balloons $= a$

Number of green balloons $= 3a$

Number of blue balloons $= 6a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 3a + 6a       &= 40 \\\\
10a               &= 40 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {40} {10} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {4 \cancel {0}} {1 \cancel {0}} \\\\
a                 &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of red balloons}      &= 4 \\\\
\text {Number of blue balloons}     &= 6a \\\\
                                    &= 6 \times 4 \\\\
                                    &= 24
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of red balloons $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of green balloons $= 3a$
<emptyline>
Number of blue balloons $= 6a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 3a + 6a       &= 40 \\\\
10a               &= 40 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {40} {10} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {4 \cancel {0}} {1 \cancel {0}} \\\\
a                 &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of red balloons}      &= 4 \\\\
\text {Number of blue balloons}     &= 6a \\\\
                                    &= 6 \times 4 \\\\
                                    &= 24
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='topics'>

talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

I think of a number. I multiply my number by $9$ and add $5$ to the result. The answer is $50$. What is my number? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 9 + 5    &= 50 \\\\
9a                &= 50 - 5 \\\\
9a                &= 45 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {45} {9} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {5 \times 9} {9} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {9}} {\cancel {9}} \\\\
a                 &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 9 + 5    &= 50 \\\\
9a                &= 50 - 5 \\\\
9a                &= 45 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {45} {9} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {5 \times 9} {9} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {9}} {\cancel {9}} \\\\
a                 &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value of a triangle $= t$

Let the value of a square $= s$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
3t + 2s     &= 16 \\\\
2t + s      &= 9 \\\\
s           &= 9 - 2t
\end {aligned}
$

We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$

$

\end {aligned}
$

**$4x - 5 = 2x + 1$**

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
4 x - 5     &= 2 x + 1 \\\\
4 x - 2 x   &= 1 + 5 \\\\
2 x         &= 6 \\\\
x           &= \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
x           &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>

Farmer John has $14$ horses and farmer Paul has $27$.

John buys some horses from Paul and then $3$ more horses than paul.

How many did John buy?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of horses John buy $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 14      &= 27 - a + 3 \\\\
a + 14      &= 30 - a \\\\
a + a       &= 30 - 14 \\\\
2a          &= 16 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {2 \times 8} {2} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 8} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a           &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>

a           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 8} {\cancel {2}} \\\\

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

The front row of a theatre has $48$ seats and each other row has four more seats than the row in front. There are $80$ seats in the last row. How many seats are there **altogether** in the theatre?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total seats}    &= 48 + 52 + 56 + 60 + 64 + 68 + 72 + 76 + 80 \\\\
                       &= 570
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$570$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total seats}    &= 48 + 52 + 56 + 60 + 64 + 68 + 72 + 76 + 80 \\\\
                       &= 570
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$570$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>FC4F6CFA-9375-4C0E-8FA7-3428B8E1BD56</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>st-albans-school--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1:1:28</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Kamu Rohith</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_kr_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prcr</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Martha and Sally each have the same amount of money.

If Martha spends $\pounds 10$ and sally spends $\pounds 20$, then Martha has three times as much as Sally.

If Martha spends $\pounds 15$ and Sally spends $\pounds 20$, then Martha has twice as much as Sally.

how much money do they have altogether?  

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let money Martha has be m and Sally has be s,

$
\begin{aligned}
m - 10 = 3 \times (s - 20) \\\\
m - 10 = 3s - 60 \\\\
3s - m = 50 \\\\ 
m =  3s -50
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
m - 15 = 2 \times (s - 20) \\\\
m - 15 = 2s - 40 \\\\
2s - m = 25 
\end{aligned}
$

Replacing m in second question

$
\begin{aligned}
2s - 3s + 50 = 25 \\\\ 
s = 25 
\end{aligned}
$

Value of m will be,
$
\begin{aligned}
m = 3s - 50 \\\\
m = 75 - 50 \\\\
m = 25 
\end{aligned}
$

Total money both has,
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 25 + 25 \\\\ 
&= \pounds 50
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let money Martha has be m and Sally has be s,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
m - 10 = 3 \times (s - 20) \\\\
m - 10 = 3s - 60 \\\\
3s - m = 50 \\\\ 
m =  3s -50
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
m - 15 = 2 \times (s - 20) \\\\
m - 15 = 2s - 40 \\\\
2s - m = 25 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Replacing m in second question
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2s - 3s + 50 = 25 \\\\ 
s = 25 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Value of m will be,
$
\begin{aligned}
m = 3s - 50 \\\\
m = 75 - 50 \\\\
m = 25 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total money both has,
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 25 + 25 \\\\ 
&= \pounds 50
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 50$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The sum of two whole numbers is 30 and their difference is 20

What are the two numbers?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let two numbers be $x$ and $y$,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + y = 30   &&&&&    x - y = 20 \\\\
x = 30 - y   &&&&&    30 - y - y = 20 \\\\
x = 30 - y   &&&&&    10 = 2y \\\\
x = 30 - y   &&&&&    y = 5 \\\\
x = 25       &&&&&    y = 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25, 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let two numbers be $x$ and $y$,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x + y = 30   &&&&&    x - y = 20 \\\\
x = 30 - y   &&&&&    30 - y - y = 20 \\\\
x = 30 - y   &&&&&    10 = 2y \\\\
x = 30 - y   &&&&&    y = 5 \\\\
x = 25       &&&&&    y = 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25, 5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

I think of a number multiply it by $3$ and then subtract $8$, if the answer is $-5$, what number did I start with? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3a - 8      &= -5 \\\\
3a          &= -5 + 8 \\\\
3a          &= 3 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {3} {3} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {\cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a           &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3a - 8      &= -5 \\\\
3a          &= -5 + 8 \\\\
3a          &= 3 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {3} {3} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {\cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a           &= 1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Nine  bus   stops are   equally  spaced   along a  bus   route.   The   distance from  the   first to the   third is $600$  metres.  How   far   is it from  the   first to the   last? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


According to question ,

Distance between first and third bus $600 \text {metres}$

Distance between first and second bus will be $300 \text {metres}$

So, distance between first and last bus will be ,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 300 \times 9 \\\\
&= 1800 \text {metres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1800 \text {metres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
According to question ,
<emptyline>
Distance between first and third bus $600 \text {metres}$
<emptyline>
Distance between first and second bus will be $300 \text {metres}$
<emptyline>
So, distance between first and last bus will be ,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 300 \times 9 \\\\
&= 1800 \text {metres}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1800 \text {metres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>C47BB056-BB47-49E9-A0DC-50131CC6D1FE</p>

<div class='papername'>
<p>st-albans-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2:1:26</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Kamu Rohith</p>

<div class='question question'>

When  asked how   many  chickens and   cows  he had   on his   farm  Mr Brown refused  to answer   directly,   but   he did   say   that  the   total number   of heads was $30$ and   the   total number   of legs  was $100$ .  How   many  of each  was   there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of chickens $=x$ and number of cows $=y$

A farmer has some chickens and some cows .He counted $30$ heads 

$ 
\begin{aligned}
x + y = 30 \\\\
x = 30 - y
\end{aligned}
$

Each chicken  has 2 legs and cow has 4 legs 

$
\begin{aligned}
2x + 4y = 100 \\\\
x  + 2y = 50 \\\\
30 - y + 2y = 50 \\\\
y = 20 \text {cows}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
x = 30 - y \\\\
x = 30 - 20 \\\\
x = 10 \text {chickens}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10 \text{chickens}, 20 \text{cows}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of chickens $=x$ and number of cows $=y$
<emptyline>
A farmer has some chickens and some cows .He counted $30$ heads 
<emptyline>
$ 
\begin{aligned}
x + y = 30 \\\\
x = 30 - y
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}

x  + 2y = 50 \\\\
30 - y + 2y = 50 \\\\
y = 20 \text {cows}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x = 30 - y \\\\
x = 30 - 20 \\\\
x = 10 \text {chickens}
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I think of a number, multiply it by $17$ and then subtract $19$. The result is $66$. What was the number I first thought of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 17 - 19        &= 66 \\\\
17a                     &= 66 + 19 \\\\
17a                     &= 85 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {85} {17} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 17} {17} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {17}} {\cancel {17}} \\\\
a                       &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

a                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 17} {17} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {17}} {\cancel {17}} \\\\
a                       &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What number must k be to make this mathematical statement true?

10  $\times$  k  +  23 = 4 $\times$  k  +  35

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
10k + 23    &= 4k + 35 \\\\
10k - 4k    &= 35 - 23 \\\\
6k          &= 12 \\\\
k           &= \dfrac {12} {6} \\\\
k           &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {6} \\\\
k           &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {6}} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
k           &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

I think of a number, divide it by $7$ and subtract $3$. The result is $6$. What was the number I first thought of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $=a$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \div 7 -3       &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {7}    &= 6 + 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {7}    &= 9 \\\\
a                 &= 9 \times 7 \\\\
a                 &= 63
\end{aligned}
$


</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

There is a number, $x$. When I add $7$ to $x$ then multiply the answer by $8$, the overall result is the same as just multiplying $x$ by 22. What is $x$? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(x + 7) \times 8  &= x \times 22 \\\\
8x + 56           &= 22x \\\\
8x - 22x          &= -56 \\\\
{}-14x            &= -56 \\\\
\cancel {-} 14x   &= \cancel {-} 56 \\\\
14x               &= 56 \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {56} {14} \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {4 \times 14} {14} \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {14}} {\cancel {14}} \\\\
x                 &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(x + 7) \times 8  &= x \times 22 \\\\
8x + 56           &= 22x \\\\
8x - 22x          &= -56 \\\\
{}-14x            &= -56 \\\\
\cancel {-} 14x   &= \cancel {-} 56 \\\\
14x               &= 56 \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {56} {14} \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {4 \times 14} {14} \\\\
x                 &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {14}} {\cancel {14}} \\\\
x                 &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>
<div class='review-comments'>

<h4>Review Comments</h4>
Start with x * 22 = (x + 7) * 8

so that there will be no need of canceling the minus sign.

For eg:

x * 22 = (x + 7) * 8

22x = 8x + 56

22x - 8x = 56

then solve it accrodingly...
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I think of a number, multiply it by $9$ and subtract $7$. The result is $101$. What was the number I first thought of? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 9 - 7    &= 101 \\\\
9a                &= 101 + 7 \\\\
9a                &= 108 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {108} {9} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {9 \times 12} {9} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 12} {\cancel {9}} \\\\
a                 &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$ 
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

\begin{aligned}

9a                &= 101 + 7 \\\\
9a                &= 108 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {108} {9} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {9 \times 12} {9} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 12} {\cancel {9}} \\\\
a                 &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

There is a number, $x$. When I add $8$ to $x$ then multiply the 
answer by $6$, the overall result is the same as just multiplying $x$ by 18. What is $x$? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
x \times 18 &= (x + 8) \times 6 \\\\
18x         &= 6x + 48 \\\\
18x - 6x    &= 48 \\\\
12x         &= 48 \\\\
x           &= \dfrac {48} {12} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{4 \times 12} {12} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel {12} } {\cancel {12} } \\\\
x           &= 4
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x \times 18 &= (x + 8) \times 6 \\\\
18x         &= 6x + 48 \\\\
18x - 6x    &= 48 \\\\
12x         &= 48 \\\\
x           &= \dfrac {48} {12} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{4 \times 12} {12} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel {12} } {\cancel {12} } \\\\
x           &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

\end {aligned}

<div class='question subquestion'>

I think of a number, divide it by $7$ and add $9$. The result is $17$. What was the number I first thought of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \div 7 + 9      &= 17 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {7}    &= 17 - 9 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {7}    &= 8 \\\\
a                 &= 8 \times 7 \\\\
a                 &= 56
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 56$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$56$

</div>
</div>

a                 &= 8 \times 7 \\\\
a                 &= 56
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 56$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

There is a number, *n*. When I add 3 to *n* then multiply the answer by 4, the overall result is the same as just multiplying *n* by 6. What is *n*?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
n \times 6  &= (n + 3) \times 4 \\\\
6n          &= (n + 3) \times 4 \\\\
6n          &= 4n + 12 \\\\
6n - 4n     &= 12 \\\\
2n          &= 12 \\\\
n           &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
n           &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
n           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
n           &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$  

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
n \times 6  &= (n + 3) \times 4 \\\\
6n          &= (n + 3) \times 4 \\\\
6n          &= 4n + 12 \\\\
6n - 4n     &= 12 \\\\
2n          &= 12 \\\\
n           &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
n           &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
n           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
n           &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$  
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
